                         DESTRUCTION NOTICE

WHY —To prevent the enemy from using or salvaging this equipment for his benefit.
WHEN—When ordered by your commander.
HOW —!1. Smash—use sledges, axes, handaxes, pickaxes, hammers, crowbars, heavy tools.

              2. Cut—Use axes, handaxes, machetes.
              3. Burn—Use gasoline, kerosene, oil, flame throwers, incendiary grenades. i
              4. Explosives Use firearms, grenades, TNT.
             5. Disposal—Bury in slit trenches, fox holes, other holes. Throw in streams. Scatter.

USE ANYTHING IMMEDIATELY AVAILABLE FOR DESTRUCTION OF THIS EQUIPMENT

WHAT—1.  Smash—Base,     covers, hood, control panels, motor, distributor disk, code disk transmitter,
         shafts, gears.

2. Cut—All power and signal cords, wiring.

         . Burn—Technical Manuals, records.

mWwe. Bury or scatter—Any or all of the above parts after they have been destroyed.

                         DESTROY EVERYTHING

                                     SAFETY NOTICE                                                  Always

   Severe shock may result from contact with current-carrying parts of this equipment.
be sure the power is disconnected before making adjustments or repairs.

Up           DESCRIPTION OF UNSATISFACTORY CONDITION: The Spring Retainer is part of the                                                                Up
             punch block assembly of the U.S. Army Signal Corps TG~26-A. Shortly after                                                                  U
U            these teletypewriters were installed, the spring retainer in three of the
   R         machines snmappea at the end section. Two were rewelded and out back in                                                                    U
             service due to lack of spares. The third was too badly broken to repair.                                                                      R

             PROBABLE CAUSES: Evidently too mucn strain is put on the arm section of this
             part especially the farther arm which is considerably smaller in width and
             thickness.

             RECOMMENDATIONS: The spring retainer should be made of non-cast or a heavier
             metal. Spares should be provided with each set.
